Fable 5 (max)claude-fable-5 — FR — FLAGSHIP. This seat is the main loop and the adjudicator. It is the session model (from the 2026-08-15 login). The seat is serviceable: after the login, the echo-test sent back the two nonces. Before the login, six tests failed because of the quota. The login made the seat serviceable. The date did not. The Kimi seat came back the same way.FR
Opus 5 (1M)claude-opus-5 — FR — The measure seat. It compiles the code and does the tests. More than 10 times, its measurements showed that the main loop was not correct. It has the same model family as the main loop before the login. Thus its approvals of that loop's work show the s badge.FR
Codex 5.6-SOL (xhigh)gpt-5.6-sol — FR — A CLI seat that can read the repository. When its session is read-only, the seat says so. Then its report is a READING, not a measurement.FR
agy 3.7 (high)gemini-3.7-flash-high — FR — The model gemini-3.7-flash-high. It reads files through --add-dir.FR
Grok 4.6 (xhigh)grok-4.6 — FR — This seat uses --prompt-file. Thus the 32 KB argument limit does not apply to it.FR
Kimi 3 (k3)kimi-code/k3 — FR+M — This seat can read files and can do tests with its own probes. On 2026-08-15 it did the adjudication alone. The FABLE/KIMI directive permits this.FR+M
glm-5.2 (cloud)glm-5.2:cloud — PK — A packet-fed seat. It gave the honest report: 'no files read; every line citation is the prompt's, re-stated'.PK
deepseek-v4-pro (cloud)deepseek-v4-pro:cloud — PK — A packet-fed seat. If the brief says 'read the source', the seat stops. That was a defect in the brief. For days, the record showed it incorrectly as a seat failure.PK
minimax-m3 (cloud)minimax-m3:cloud — PK — A packet-fed seat. The record showed it as unserviceable for days. When a brief contained all the necessary facts, the seat gave a 12 KB answer.PK
n2n2 timer.c: no upper limit on a requested timer frequency (panel discovery)An2 timer.c: no upper limit on a requested timer frequency (panel discovery) — commits: 863d238 b9daca1 cdc4849 — HOLD: reviewed; regression batched to Sunday night — REVIEWED AND FOLLOWED UP: 863d238 (round) + b9daca1 (records) + cdc4849 (follow-up). Assessment (initial problem)✓2026-08-15 — MANUAL — REAL - the missing clamp is real AND the symptom of a deeper modeling error: a ONE-SHOT deadline delta is converted into a PERIODIC frequency. Clamp = blast-radius control; the deadline-as-frequency model is a design item to FILE, not to fix in this batch. — evidence: pipeline/evidence/_dispatches/fable5_n1n4_2026-08-15.md✓2026-08-15 — CONFIRMED — REAL - measured: finite burst of 615,384.5 callbacks per signal (= 4e7/s); no absorption before 2^28 s; the lag resync amplifies bursts (1 s lag -> 20.3M). Also: timer.c:137 stores the unclamped freq. Not MIPS-only - see n3, n4. — evidence: pipeline/evidence/n2/assess/opus_2026-08-15.md✓2026-08-15 — MANUAL — REAL - source-verified. Nuance: the HOST burst needs only emulated_hz>0, so default pmax permits it; only the guest interrupt storm needs non-EXC3K. — evidence: pipeline/evidence/n2/assess/codex_2026-08-15.md✓2026-08-15 — MANUAL — REAL - source-verified. vocore is the worst default: 3.6e8 Hz -> ~5.5M iterations per signal. Research (fix proposals)✓2026-08-16 — MANUAL — REFRAMED THE ROUND: the panel's 300:1 spread on the ceiling is itself evidence that A FIXED CEILING IS THE WRONG INSTRUMENT - the measured harm is iterations per signal, and even the largest proposed ceiling leaves ~153,846 of them. The catch-up CAP is the protection; the ceiling is domain normalisation. Also: the report belongs at the CLAMP SITE (device context, where fatal() is already idiom), not in the handler - and the SIGALRM hazard codex worried about does not exist there. NaN is caught by NEGATING the comparisons that must exist anyway, at zero marginal cost: A FREE GUARD AT A TRUST BOUNDARY NEEDS NO REACHABILITY PROOF. Committed against shipping the loop bound alone, because interval=0 would then burn the cap every signal for ever. — evidence: pipeline/evidence/n2/research/fable5_2026-08-16.md✓2026-08-16 — CONFIRMED — MEASURED EVERYTHING, AND OVERTURNED TWO PROPOSALS. Built the offline driver and proved timer.c CAN be driven in the diff_*.c idiom (one stub: gettimeofday). CENSUS: emulated_hz is an int, so INT_MAX bounds every legitimate request - the ceiling is DERIVED, not picked. POLICY TABLE: 'drop missed ticks' is NOT A POLICY BUT A DEFECT (it caps every timer at 65 Hz - a 100 Hz timer runs at rate 0.65 with no burst at all); the flagship's cap of 65536 BREAKS a legitimate 4e7 Hz timer (1.0000 -> 0.1065); 2^20 is the smallest power of two that leaves every legitimate case BIT-IDENTICAL to shipped. And clamping at :137 alone is not just insufficient but A REGRESSION - it loses idempotence on five of six classes because the early-out then compares a clamped record against a raw request. Caught its own first probe (it held time fixed and would have cleared the defect). — evidence: pipeline/evidence/n2/research/opus_measured_2026-08-16.md✓2026-08-16 — MANUAL — NORMALISE AND REPORT excessive rates rather than silently clamping; guard intervals; bound the catch-up; clamp RTC values BEFORE narrowing to int. The only seat to make reporting the headline - which matches the ethos that a silently absorbed wrong value is worse than a loud one. — evidence: pipeline/evidence/n2/research/codex_2026-08-16.md✓2026-08-16 — MANUAL — clamp to [1e-8, 1e5] Hz; fix the :137 store ordering; guard interval against NaN and zero; bound the catch-up. — evidence: pipeline/evidence/n2/research/agy_2026-08-16.md✓2026-08-16 — MANUAL — ONE helper clamps into [1e-8, 32768], stores only after the clamp, snaps a 1024-iteration catch-up, and sanitises the rtc value. The lowest bound proposed. — evidence: pipeline/evidence/n2/research/grok_2026-08-16.md✓2026-08-16 — MANUAL — clamp to [1e-8, 1e7] with NAN-SAFE COMPARISONS at both entry points, storing the clamped value. The NaN point is load-bearing: `freq <= 1e-8` is FALSE for NaN, so a NaN passes both clamps untouched today. — evidence: pipeline/evidence/n2/research/kimi_2026-08-16.md✓2026-08-16 — BELIEVED — packet: clamp to [1e-8, 1e7] at entry to both functions; guard interval INDEPENDENTLY of freq. — evidence: pipeline/evidence/n2/research/glm_2026-08-16.md✓2026-08-16 — BELIEVED — packet: a FINITE 10 MHz upper bound; guard interval after the division; bound the catch-up with a resync; store the clamped value. — evidence: pipeline/evidence/n2/research/deepseek_2026-08-16.md✓2026-08-16 — BELIEVED — packet: clamp to an IN-TREE-JUSTIFIED bound (declining to invent a number without the callers) and fix the :137 pre-clamp store. — evidence: pipeline/evidence/n2/research/minimax_2026-08-16.md

hlenhlen Device handlers that ignore the access length (audit)hlen Device handlers that ignore the access length (audit) — HOLD: census done; promote to a FIX round? — Opened from the b75 refutation, then characterized by census. A device handler that switches on the address and cannot service a width will read or write only the first register of an 8-byte access. MEASURED SCOPE: 62 spanning-capable handlers of 167 registered; 20 on machines this project boots. TWO BOOTED MACHINES ARE PROVABLY IMMUNE - SH-4 has no 8-byte access at all (fmov.d is two 4-byte accesses) and MIPS32/R3000A treats LD/SD as reserved, so landisk and pmax cannot produce the access. NO CONVENTION EXISTS in the tree: no helper, no macro, no rule - only three hand-rolled decompositions that are all big-endian-specific and that disagree with each other about the else branch, so there is no single idiom to copy. FIRST WITNESS NAMED: dev_irqc on testarm, the only spanning-capable device on that machine, where the two halves of one aligned 8-byte access have OPPOSITE legal directions (status is read-only, mask is write-only), which makes the miss unambiguous. DETECTOR SHAPE: an offline differential in the diff_*.c family - no boot, no rig, no serialization.held

[panel_20260817_112300]…2026-08-17 — THE ADVERSARIAL FINDING, and it BOUNDS THE ROUND: the settled two-arm doctrine has a MISSING THIRD ARM, and a mechanical sweep would ship a worse defect than it removes. * sh4_dmac_transfer (dev_sh4.c:340-428, DEVICE_ACCESS-reachable, exits at :372/:381/:390/:422/:427): the ONLY implemented CHCR_RS mode is 0x200, which by its own comment transfers nothing; there is no memory-to-memory path at all. Apply complain-and-continue and a guest programming ordinary dual-address DMA gets NO BYTES MOVED, CHCR_TE never set, no interrupt -- it polls TE forever or reads a destination that was never written. Silent corruption or a silent hang surfacing in a driver far from the DMAC, strictly HARDER to diagnose than today's exit(1), which names the mode in one line. * AND THE LATCH MAKES IT WORSE, NOT BETTER: for a hot STATUS register latching is right, but for a STATEFUL OPERATION the first dropped transfer reports and every later one is invisible -- the latch actively SUPPRESSES the evidence of accumulating divergence. That inverts the #433 refinement instead of extending it. * Same class at sh4_pcic's window-base registers (PCIMBR :917, PCIIOBR :926, PCILAR0/1 :891/:909, PCILSR0/1 :882/:900): dropping a write leaves the emulator decoding at the OLD base while the guest believes it moved, so every later PCI access lands at the wrong address -- possibly on a different device that answers. * NOT A NEW CLASS -- #433's own adjudication hit it once (FLUSH_SUPER_LINE: a silently ignored TLB flush leaves the guest translating through a torn-down mapping) and solved it LOCALLY, without writing the rule down. So a mechanical pass over 170 sites would ship it at least three more times. * THE ARM, stated so it applies per site: complain-and-continue is safe ONLY when the dropped write's only observable consequence is the register's own value. If the write's PURPOSE is to change how the emulator decodes, translates, transfers or invalidates -- address windows, TLB/cache flushes, DMA descriptors, coherency ops -- dropping it makes the model LIE rather than LAG, and the guest cannot tell. For that class: implement it, or stop honestly with cpu->running = 0 (which unlike exit(1) preserves the debugger and the emulator's own diagnostics) -- NEVER a latched drop. Three meanings, not two. * AND THE ARM VINDICATES THE ROUND'S OWN SITE RATHER THAN BLOCKING IT: INT_ST_MASK IS state-changing (it is an SPL write), BUT the guarded portion -- bits 25..0 -- is MEASURED DEAD, so store-as-is drops nothing and the latched shape is correct at :816. That is exactly why this one site is safe and a blanket sweep is not. [panel_20260817_112300]…2026-08-17 — [HELD AWAITING SEAT: codex] QUOTA WALL, measured not assumed. It launched (banner, gpt-5.6-sol, xhigh, read-only sandbox), echoed the 9,375-byte brief, and ended 'ERROR: You've hit your usage limit ... try again at Aug 22nd, 2026 12:12 PM'. NO REVIEW. panel.sh scored it '10063b answered' and printed SEATS 7/7; I then told the owner codex was alive on the strength of that banner. The banner proves the CLI STARTED. Missing: fable5, agy, grok, kimi, glm, deepseek, minimax. — evidence: policy annotation, not a review✓2026-08-15 — CONFIRMED — UNBLOCKED: the 21285 datasheet was obtained (_scratchpad/dc21285.pdf, Intel 278115-001). Four defects are now specifiable. (1) SS7.3.41 bit 6: FREERUN wraps to FFFFFFh and keeps counting; PERIODIC reloads TimerNLoad; the clock source is bits 3:2 alone, identical in both. reload_timer_value() computes one rate and hands it to a repeating timer -- the PERIODIC cadence unconditionally -- so a FREERUN timer is too fast by up to 2^24/load. (2) TimerNValue is READ-ONLY (SS7.3.38/7.3.40) but the model accepts a guest write, and TIMER_1_CLEAR has NO writeflag test, so a guest READ of a write-only register reloads the counter and deasserts the IRQ. (3) Reset values diverge: hardware gives CONTROL 0x00 and LOAD 0, the model gives TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC and TIMER_MAX_VAL -- and this is the model bug #432's first draft mistakenly cited as evidence for its own zero mapping. (4) The random() counter model is now fully specified by SS7.3.40 + SS7.3.41 bit 7: a 24-bit down-counter, readable any time, RETAINED across disable and resuming on re-enable. The author's TODO is discharged; only a rig is missing. — evidence: _scratchpad/panel_20260815_221232/✓2026-08-16 — MANUAL — Expand the item: external-event handling, reserved CONTROL-bit masking, and the retained/down-counter model belong in it alongside the four already listed. Ordering it after i8253zero would also be defensible, since i8253zero has a direct datasheet answer. — evidence: _scratchpad/panel_20260815_230954/codex.txt······

i8253zeroi8253zero A programmed count of 0 means 65536, not 'disabled' -- now datasheet-backedi8253zero A programmed count of 0 means 65536, not 'disabled' -- now datasheet-backed — HOLD: settled by document; one-line fix in the hz computationheld
Assessment (initial problem)·✓2026-08-15 — CONFIRMED — SETTLED FROM A REAL DOCUMENT, which is the whole point of the item. i8253reg.h says nothing about a zero count (read in full, 102 lines), so the belief rested on memory -- exactly the trap this project names. The Intel 8254 datasheet (_scratchpad/i8254.pdf, 231164-005) states it twice on p.17: 'The largest possible initial count is 0; this is equivalent to 2^16 for binary counting and 10^4 for BCD counting.' So dev_8253.c:125's `else hz = 0` -- which the timer core then floor-clamps to 1e-8 Hz, i.e. never fires -- is wrong against the hardware convention. Cite it as the 8254, NOT the 8253: the count-0 convention was verified in the 8254 document only. — evidence: _scratchpad/panel_20260815_221232/·······

fbpendingfbpending footbridge pending_timer_interrupts is an uncapped int, never decremented by the tickfbpending footbridge pending_timer_interrupts is an uncapped int, never decremented by the tick — HOLD: same class as #432, same registers; not a regressionheld
Assessment (initial problem)·✓2026-08-15 — MANUAL — From R6 pass 2 (measuring seat, MANUAL). pending_timer_interrupts[] is a plain int, incremented from the SIGALRM handler with NO cap, and DEVICE_TICK never decrements it -- only TIMER_x_CLEAR (by one) and the disable path do. On cats (emulated_hz 50 MHz) a LEGAL load=1 with ENABLE accrues ~5e7/s and reaches signed-int overflow in ~43 s; four timers at the catch-up ceiling do it in ~7.9 s. NOT a regression -- the same was reachable before #432 -- but it is the same class as the bug just fixed, in the same registers, and #432's own text computes the 31 s figure while attributing it to a REJECTED design rather than to the shipped one. — evidence: _scratchpad/panel_20260815_230954/·······

m8probem8probe m8820x PROBE_USER/PROBE_SUPER are dropped, so SSR answers a deterministic missm8probe m8820x PROBE_USER/PROBE_SUPER are dropped, so SSR answers a deterministic miss — HOLD: no measured guest issues probes; needs the translation walkheld
Assessment (initial problem)✓2026-08-16 — CONFIRMED — From R7. After #433 a probe is complained-once and dropped, and the guest then reads regs[CMMU_SSR], which nothing ever writes -- the model writes only PFSR/PFAR (memory_m88k.c:382-405) -- so probes deterministically answer 0, i.e. V-bit clear, a defined 'no valid translation' rather than a plausible-looking valid answer. The measured OpenBSD/luna88k boot issues NO probes (7 command values, all handled); NetBSD and MP kernels are unmeasured. Real semantics mean reusing the existing translation walk against regs[CMMU_SAR] and synthesising SSR from the header's bit definitions (m8820x.h:155-163). No 88200 manual exists in-tree, so where a bit's setting condition is not derivable from the header plus the existing walk, leave it 0 and record UNKNOWN. — evidence: _scratchpad/panel_20260815_234456/········

m8segm8seg A SEGMENT flush maps to ALL, and the over-invalidation is guest-visiblem8seg A SEGMENT flush maps to ALL, and the over-invalidation is guest-visible — HOLD: Behaviour change, not a records fix; the shipped direction is the SAFE one (under-invalidation is what leaves a stale translation). Assessment (initial problem)····✓2026-08-16 — CONFIRMED — The fold sends CMMU_FLUSH_{USER,SUPER}_SEGMENT to the all=1 arm. Safe direction, but NOT free of guest-visible effect, and the shipped comment claimed it was. A walk writes PG_U (and PG_M on a write) back into the page descriptor IN EMULATED MEMORY; a PATC hit sets those bits only in the PATC copy. So a guest that clears U on a page in segment B, flushes segment A, then touches B observes a U bit a correctly-scoped flush would have left clear. The tighter mapping needs no manual: match the walker's own 4 MB segment size, (sar ^ vaddr) & 0xffc00000. What real silicon does at segment granularity is UNKNOWN -- there is no 88200/88204 manual in this project. — evidence: memory_m88k.c:356-365 vs :348-350 (read, not inferred)····

Honest UNKNOWN: Linux >=4.13 reports an fsync failure ONCE and drops the dirty pages, so a LATER sync returns success after data was already lost. * Q3 DO NOT sync d->f under overlays, and it is worse than pointless: diskimage.c:1984 opens d->f in mode r whenever prefix_R is set while :1971 sets writable=1, so R: -- WHAT BOTH RIGS USE -- gives a writable disk whose base handle is READ-ONLY, and the temp overlay is already unlinked. fflush on a read-mode stream is UB in C99 7.21.5.2p2; fsync on O_RDONLY is rc=0 on glibc but UNKNOWN on NetBSD/OpenBSD/Solaris, where it would turn every R:/CD-ROM sync into a spurious MEDIUM ERROR. It BUILT that state and drove the shipping read path: the guest reads 0x00 from the overlay's HOLE and the base's good 0xBB bytes are GONE -- silent corruption of data the guest never touched, reported as a successful read. It does NOT fall through to the base. Honest counterweight: NOT reproducible in the current write path (BIT_SET_BUT_DATA_MISSING=0 across n=1..129; the per-block fseeks keep them in lockstep), so it is a hole the FIX would INTRODUCE, not a live defect; (iii) directory-entry durability CHECKED AND NOT A HOLE -- user overlays open r+ and must pre-exist, and the only created file is the unlinked temp overlay. An honest negative. * Also found: there is NO fclose of d->f or the overlays at shutdown anywhere. A clean exit() flushes stdio so a normal quit is safe, but a SIGKILLed emulator loses the tail regardless. * ITS OWN ERROR, reported rather than dropped: its first detector left c.nblk UNINITIALIZED, so every overlay row went red -- AGREEING WITH ITS HYPOTHESIS -- and at -O2 malloc(512*garbage) SIGSEGVd into 4 FAULTS it nearly reported as an overlay-path defect. [panel_20260817_215052]…2026-08-17 — *** NAMED A MEASUREMENT NOBODY HAD RUN AND TOLD ME TO RUN IT BEFORE WRITING THE CHANGELOG SENTENCE: an fflush-ONLY (no fsync) arm. Its words: 'If that arm persists after _exit, the kill-and-check row is not an fsync test.' I RAN IT AND IT WAS RIGHT. *** MEASURED, one 512-byte write: fflush only -> 512 bytes DURABLE to a separate reader; fsync only (what ships today) -> 0. So _exit() simulates PROCESS death, not host death -- the page cache belongs to the KERNEL and outlives the process -- and a kill-and-check test therefore measures the missing FFLUSH, never the missing FSYNC. This SPLITS THE DEFECT: (a) missing fflush is byte-testable and bounded; (b) overlays never fsynced at all is NOT byte-testable by ANY process-kill test, needs the --wrap rows, and is unbounded on real power loss. Seven more footbridge sites sit in the same 0x400 window. * *** THE PROPOSED ANTI-LAUNDERING CLAUSE REJECTS THE PROJECT'S OWN EXEMPLAR. *** regress/m8820x_sites_probe.py states the panel's governing principle VERBATIM and is where the principle comes from -- yet it shipped as 9494c6a, THREE COMMITS AFTER the #433 fix 66b0367 (verified), with every row SURVIVED, post-fix only. It satisfies neither clause (1) 'defect symptom on the pre-fix HEAD' nor clause (2) 'pre-fix failure and post-fix success'. The principle was lifted out of a DETECTOR and is being written as a rule for WITNESSES. * SO THE DETECTOR/WITNESS DISTINCTION IS NOT NEW -- it is committed, in that file's own comment, together with its cost argument (3.2 s cold-debugger vs 182-233 s for a rig drive, ~60x cheaper). Where the project conflates them is not in its ENGINEERING but in its RECORDS: round prose says 'reproduced' without naming the artefact. * A MISSING RUNG, and it names where laundering will actually happen: the real ladder is (1) #include harness -- behaviour only, rejected; (2) MACHINE CONSTRUCTION -- device registered in the address map, no guest instruction, WHICH THE RULE IS SILENT ABOUT and gate 9 already does for every machine type; (3) cold-debugger probe -- real instruction, real decode; (4) boot -- a real driver reaches it unaided. * DROP 'EQUAL': a probe proves a path CAN be reached (right for a bounds fix); a boot proves it IS travelled (right for RANKING). #437 is the illustration -- its luna88k boot showed SYNCHRONIZE CACHE is issued once per boot right after MARKING FILE SYSTEM CLEAN, which no probe could produce. Admissible-equal, subordinate for prioritisation. * RETROACTIVE COST: ZERO closed rows are invalidated (85 rows, 16 closed, scanned). But the check is weaker than it looks and THE REAL FINDING IS A SCHEMA GAP -- the ledger has no field naming WHICH ARTEFACT WITNESSED THE DEFECT, so the question is answerable only by reading prose. b47/#47 is the closest call: its defect witness is the #include differential ('15 of 23 red against the pre-change file') and its boots are no-regression checks, not defect witnesses. * STUBBING DEMONSTRABLY DISTORTS: m8820x's offline reproduction reported 1013/1015/11 where the true numbers are 1007/1009/17 -- a stub artefact, because the harness left invalidate_translation_caches NULL and that callback fires on reads too. The one round that reproduced offline got its numbers wrong, in the direction the principle predicts. * AGREES exitsweep stays at ONE SITE, but on a different and stronger ground: not 'only one is reproducible' (false) but the THIRD DOCTRINE ARM, which bounds it on SEMANTICS and does not evaporate when someone writes a probe. Proposed reopening rule: add a second site only when it is witnessed, in the SAME DEVICE and SAME SEMANTIC CLASS so one fix design covers both, and covered by the SAME DETECTOR without a new oracle -- under which the eight footbridge sites are ONE candidate round, not eight. Assessment (initial problem)✓2026-08-16 — CONFIRMED — From R7. dev_m8820x.c's PFSR/PFAR/SAR/SCTR/SAPR/UAPR arm calls invalidate_translation_caches(c, 0, INVALIDATE_ALL) BEFORE the writeflag check, so plain READS purge every translation. PFSR is read on fault paths, so this may fire per-fault for a whole boot. THIS IS ALSO THE MECHANISM THAT CORRUPTED THE FIRST REPRODUCTION -- the offline driver left that callback NULL and six offsets crashed in the harness. Test-first: instrument a counter across a full luna88k boot on a scratch copy; if ~0, close as 'assessed, not changed'. If hot, move the invalidate under the writeflag check -- reads have no architectural invalidation semantics to preserve, and keeping it on writes preserves every existing correctness cue. — evidence: _scratchpad/panel_20260815_234456/········
Research (fix proposals)✓2026-08-16 — CONFIRMED — REPRODUCED TEST-FIRST, 4 rows / 0 failures, REPRO_M8INVREAD_CONFIRMED. dev_m8820x.c:304-314 groups PFSR, PFAR, SAR, SCTR, SAPR and UAPR and calls invalidate_translation_caches(c, 0, INVALIDATE_ALL) UNCONDITIONALLY, one line ABOVE the `if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)` that stores -- so each of the six purges the emulator's entire dyntrans mapping on a plain READ. Measured 1 purge per register, 6 of 6, counted through an installed no-op callback rather than inferred. THREE CONTROLS HOLD, and they are what make it a measurement rather than a reading: the WRITE side still purges 6 (deliberately not under test), reading IDR from outside the group purges 0 (so the count is this arm and not something ambient), and the read still returns the seeded value 0xabcd (so a later fix cannot pass by turning the read into a no-op). WHY IT IS NOT MERELY UNTIDY: PFSR and PFAR are what a fault handler READS to find out what faulted and where, so the cost lands on the ordinary page-fault path, twice per fault. Per-boot read count NOT yet measured. THIS IS THE SAME CALLBACK whose NULLness corrupted the first m8820x reproduction by exactly six each way -- that accident was this defect seen from the other side. — evidence: regress/repro_m8invread.c (offline differential, #includes the shipped .c)········

Research (fix proposals)·…2026-08-19 — BUILT AND CLOSED, and it IMPROVED ON THE RECIPE IT WAS GIVEN. Told to build the single-overlay row a prior pass had specified, it built that, then probed two SIBLING mutants and measured that the single-overlay row catches ONLY the deletion: `continue` -> `break;` and `continue` -> `return 0;` both pass it GREEN, because with one overlay there is no next iteration and all three mutants trace identically. A SECOND OVERLAY separates them and one row kills all three. It supplied both patches and recommended the stronger, which also perturbs no existing gate check where the fallback would have forced one from "1" to "2". * Mechanism: __wrap_fsync consults a static fail_fsync_fd, sets errno=EIO and returns -1 without calling __real_fsync; armed after the WRITE, disarmed when the command returns. It rejected, with reasons written into the file, a real failing fsync (needs dm-error/root, non-portable -- same class as the drvfs trap), close()ing the fd under the live FILE* (a FAULT, and can hit a recycled fd) and an O_RDONLY fd (rc 0 on glibc, proves nothing). * A CRASH IS NOT WHAT MAKES IT FAIL, measured four ways: rc is 1 never >=128; the mutant printed all 11 rows plus the verdict line so it reached the end of main; ASan+UBSan produced 0 bytes on both arms; and exactly one row differs, by a printed string. * INSTRUMENT-SABOTAGE CONTROL (`if (0 && fd == fail_fsync_fd)`) turns TWO rows red, so a dead injection fails loudly and the signatures are distinguishable: two red together = the instrument, the ordering row red alone = the code. * VERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY BY THE MAIN LOOP, and MY FIRST THREE ATTEMPTS WERE ALL VOID -- the loop variable was eaten before reaching WSL and the printed rc was grep's status; then the anchor took 'the first continue in a range' when the loop has TWO and the f_data==NULL branch never executes, so all three mutants 'passed' and would have discredited the row; then anchoring on the fsync call matched :1211 in overlay_set_block_in_use, a different function. Only a context printout of the line about to be mutated caught it. Correctly anchored on the unique 'Data is not durable' comment: ALL THREE MUTANTS KILLED, traces distinguishable -- deleted publishes b0 anyway, break/return0 stop at F(d0) S(d0). * Gate 2: PASS 235 checks (was 231). Honest limits recorded in the file: the CHECK-CONDITION row is a WITNESS not a discriminator (green on every code mutant); an fflush failure on the data file is not injected, only fsync; and the injection skips the real fsync, so a partially-successful sync is UNTESTED and UNKNOWN. ✓ approved ⏸ held / in work ✕ objection … no answer / seat failure (not agreement) · no input — input is possible A the author did this review (shown) s a review by the same model family FR = reads files · FR+M = reads files and does tests · PK = gets a packet only
The program gen_dashboard.py makes this page from pipeline/ledger.json. Do not change this page manually. If a cell does not have a ledger entry, the cell is a defect. A packet-fed seat examines the argument only. Its vote about the contents of the repository is not equal to the vote of a seat that reads files (PIPELINE.md, Section 1). An ✕ cell can be the most important cell in its row: the ✕ in the Research phase of #419 changed the design. Rule for closed rows (owner, 2026-08-15): a closed row does not need new tests when the flagship seat (Fable) examined the final change and its evidence. That examination fills the Final review cell of the closed row. Regression phase (owner, 2026-08-15): only the flagship seat reviews the regression tests, and the reviews are BATCHED TO SUNDAY NIGHT, after the full battery, which now runs Sunday at 18:00 so that its evidence covers the rounds the review examines. The other eight cells in a Regression line are NOT REQUIRED, and a Regression line that is empty on a round finished after Saturday is WAITING FOR THE SUNDAY BATCH, not missing. So a line of dots there shows a rule, not a gap. The first three phases still need all nine seats, per round, before that round proceeds.
